# Nepal — English books

**NEPAL OPENED 2026-11-03 under the user's
all-closed-countries ruling.** Nepal is CLOSED in
the library. Its recorded English set is 18 books —
12 student's books (Grades 1–12) and 6 teacher's
guides (Grades 1, 2, 3, 6, 9, 10) — all 18 in hand,
0 link-only. All 18 files were pulled from the
secured GitHub library byte-exact against the record
(82 MB; 3,448 PDF pages in total), and every cover
and title page was checked against its record row:
**the true count is the recorded 18 — 18 files, 18
distinct books, no duplicates, no swaps, and no
extra Nepal English books at legacy paths** (the
library record holds exactly 18 Nepal English rows).

**The headline finding: the teacher's guides mostly
do NOT belong to the held student's books.** Of the
six recorded guides, only Grade 3's is a true
companion to its held student's book. The Grade 1
guide (First Edition 2020) and the Grade 2 guide
("Frist edition: 2021" [sic]) are Ministry of
Education, Science and Technology
integrated-curriculum guides to newer workbooks
that are not held — zero content overlap with the
held Grade 1 and Grade 2 books. The Grade 6 guide
(Edition 2022) keys to a newer eighteen-unit Grade
6 textbook, not the held 2012 book. The Grade 9
guide is a DRAFT written for an older twenty-unit
textbook, and the held copy is an incomplete
excerpt of it. The Grade 10 guide (First Edition
2024; its title page is marked "Feedback Copy")
keys to a newer Grade 10 edition, not the held
2017 book — despite its own Preface claiming it
"is fully based on the contents and tasks of Grade
10 English textbook". The held student's books are
mostly the older generation: Grades 1–5 are the
"MY ENGLISH BOOK" series (first editions
2003–2007, reprints 2010/2011), Grade 6 is the
1994 book in its 2012 revised edition, and the
secondary books are Grade 7 (First Edition 2022,
Updated Edition 2025), Grade 8 (First Edition
2023, Updated Edition 2025), Grade 9 (First
Edition 2022), Grade 10 (First Edition 2017),
Grade 11 (Edition 2077 BS = 2020 AD) and Grade 12
(First Edition 2021).

**Record corrections proved by covers and
interiors.** (1) Grade 9 student's book: the
record's "2079" appears nowhere in the book — the
imprint prints "First Edition: 2022" only. (2)
Grade 12: the record's "(Compulsory)" appears
nowhere as a title element; the book is simply
"English Grade 12". (3) Grade 7: the record's
"Final" ("Final Updated Edition 2025") is printed
nowhere — the imprint reads "First Edition: 2022 /
Updated Edition: 2025" (the updated-edition claim
itself is true as printed; Grade 8's imprint reads
the same way for 2023/2025). (4) Grade 4 prints
"Grade IV" and Grade 5 "Grade V" where the record
says Grade 4 and Grade 5. (5) Grade 10 student's
book: the title page reads "ENGLISH Class 10"
(cover: "ENGLISH Grade 10"), and its imprint
prints "ISBN:" followed by nothing — no ISBN
exists in the book. (6) Grade 11's printed title
forms are "English Grade 11" (cover) and "English
(Grade 11)" (title page); the record's "English
Grade Eleven" matches neither.

**Copy defects in the secured files.** The Grade 5
student's book is INCOMPLETE: printed pp.21–30
(the whole of Unit 3, "Describing locations") are
missing, and the Listening texts listed in its
Contents at p.107 are missing too (the file ends
at printed p.106). The Grade 6 student's book is
TRUNCATED at printed p.108 — Units 13–17, the
Listening script (Contents p.151) and the
Vocabulary (pp.i–v) are not in the file — and
MISBOUND: printed pp.27–37 (Unit 4) are bound
before pp.20–26 (Unit 3), so its section states
four segment offsets instead of one. The Grade 9
teacher's guide is an INCOMPLETE EXCERPT: printed
pp.1–25 (Units 1–2), 55–61 (Unit 7), 75–81 (Unit
10) and 97–103 (Unit 13) are missing entire. The
Grade 3 teacher's guide is image-only and was read
from the PDF's own embedded page images.

**ISBN state.** An ISBN is printed in five
student's books: Grade 1 978-999-33-714-0-8, Grade
2 978-999-33-708-7-8, Grade 3 978-999-33-708-9-4,
Grade 4 978-999-33-714-1-6, Grade 6
978-9937-601-00-9. Grade 5 prints its ISBN
unfinished — "978-999-33-717-.......", the last
digits as dots (verified on the rendered imprint).
Grade 10 prints a blank ISBN line. The other
eleven books print no ISBN at all.

**Honest series verdicts (each is stated in the
book sections below).** There is no examination
preparation anywhere in the held set: the Grade
10, 11 and 12 books were checked whole — no SEE or
NEB past papers, no model question sets, no exam
strategy, no marking schemes — and no held guide
carries any either; the primary books have no
tests or revision units at all. There is no
systematic phonics programme in any primary book.
No student's book prints answer keys (the only
exceptions: Grade 5's upside-down Tricky talk
answers and Grade 10's riddle answers, p.215).
The present perfect is never taught in any held
primary book, and not as a topic in Grade 7 either
(forms occur untaught inside its readings). The
passive voice is first taught in Grade 6 (present
and past simple), is then absent entirely from
Grade 7, and is taught once in Grade 9.
Conditionals arrive only in Grade 9 — types 1, 2
and 3; the type 0 is never named. Reported speech
is promised by Grade 6's Contents in its missing
Unit 13, first taught in Grade 7 (statements
only), assembled in full only in Grade 8, and
split across three widely separated units in
Grade 9.

**Citation check: 393 anchors — 381 passed exactly
as cited, 12 corrected in place, 0 not found.**
One reader claim was disproved by its checker and
is corrected in the filing below: the Grade 6
student's Contents DOES list Unit 12 ("12. George
Stephenson … 102") — the coordinator verified the
row directly in the book.

## Grade 1 — MY ENGLISH BOOK Grade I (student's book)

### Identity block
- Title as printed: cover "MY ENGLISH BOOK / Grade I"; the running footer throughout is "MY ENGLISH BOOK: Grade I". The record calls it "My English Book Grade 1" — the printed grade form is the Roman numeral "Grade I", and the printed title has no separate title page (PDF p.1 is the cover, PDF p.2 is the imprint page).
- Publisher: Government of Nepal, Ministry of Education, Curriculum Development Centre, Sanothimi, Bhaktapur (cover and imprint, PDF pp.1–2). The Preface is signed for "Ministry of Education and Sports" (PDF p.3) — a ministry-name difference inside the same book.
- Year / edition / ISBN as printed: ISBN 978-999-33-714-0-8 (imprint). "First edition: 2003 / Reprint: 2011". The imprint also carries the CDC approval note for printing and distribution by private printers in the thirty-two districts of the Eastern and Western Development Regions from academic year 2068 BS under the 'School Textbooks and Reading Materials Printing and Distribution Directives - 2067 BS'. Price line is printed blank ("Price: Rs." with dotted lines for printer/distributor names, to be filled by the local printer).
- Developers/authors as printed: no cover authors. The Preface (PDF p.3) states the book was "originally written by Ranjana Bajagain and revised by Arun Kiran Pradhan, Christine Stone, Maya Rai and Bishnu Prasad Parajuli in 2003"; layout design by Navindra Rajbhandari and Himalaya Gautam; illustrations by Abindra Man Shrestha.
- Curriculum/series notes: the first book of the CDC primary English series, written for the government's introduction of English from Grade One starting academic year 2003 (Preface). The Preface states the focus for this grade is "particularly on listening and speaking skills". Most pages carry short teacher directions in Nepali (printed in a Devanagari font that extracts as mojibake in the text layer but is legible on the page) — the pupil's book doubles as a teacher-prompt book.
- Extent: 72 PDF pages = cover (PDF 1) + imprint (PDF 2) + Preface (PDF 3) + Contents (PDF 4) + printed pp.1–68 (PDF 5–72). Printed p.68 is the second Word list page; there is no back cover in the file.
- Printed↔PDF offset: printed = PDF − 4. Established from the Contents (Unit 1 at printed p.1 = PDF 5) and the printed folios, which match this offset on every page from p.1 (PDF 5) to p.68 (PDF 72).

### Unit-by-unit summary
- Unit 1 Starting school (pp.1–6): greetings and introductions — "Good morning, Miss", "I'm Miss Thapa / I'm Raju", asking names ("Hello, what's your name?", "What's her/his name?", she's/he's), naming classroom things (bag, ruler, book) with it's a…, classroom commands (stand up, sit down), a hello chant ("How are you today? — I'm fine"), drawing straight lines, and a circle-the-same-one matching exercise. First letter-shape work is implicit only.
- Unit 2 Sounds and letter shapes (pp.7–14): initial-sound word sets grouped by letter — l (lamp, leaf, leg, ladder), t (tap, table, toe, tree, tin), i (ink), c (cap, carrot, cat, cow) — with this/that, "What's this/that?", do this/do that, the game "Run to the board", chants, and four-line copy work with stroke directions ("down", "down and across", "down and dot", "round").
- Unit 3 Giving commands and making requests (pp.15–21; contents shortens the title to "Giving commands"): commands and requests with please (point to, touch, close the window, open your book, pick up, put down), a commands chant, an/a with orange, apple, elephant, egg, and body words in my/his/her frames ("Touch your eye — This is my eye", "Point to his mouth", "Touch her nose").
- Unit 4 Talking together (pp.22–28): yes/no questions "Is this/that a…? — Yes, it is / No, it isn't", a guessing chant (finger/window/hen/pen), this/these and that/those with nail/nails and net/nets, "What are these? — They are nets", a guessing game, fan/fish contrasts, and initial-letter completion (_lag, _lower, _ish).
- Unit 5 One and many (pp.29–35): counting rhyme 1–10, "How many fingers are there?", there is/there are with counted nouns (birds, girls, boys, ball, houses, trees), his/her house, a number chant (3 flags, 4 pens … 10 cats), listen-and-draw with numbers, the letter m set (mango, mat, mouth, moon), and singular/plural question-and-answer ("What's this? It's a rat / What are these? They are rats", "How many rats are there? There are three rats").
- Unit 6 Where things are (pp.36–43): prepositions on, in, under with where's…? questions, here/there/everywhere chant, letters o and g work, put-your-book-in/on/under commands, a chalk-hunt guessing game, reading word rows, my/your ("This is my book / That's your bag"), the letter b set (bag, banana, bus, ball, box), word completion (_lass, _tar, _lag, _us, _anana, _ag), and his/her with pen/pencil, pan/pot.
- Unit 7 Numbers and colours (pp.44–50): "One potato, two potatoes" counting, "a lot of potatoes", colour words (blue, red, black, green, yellow, brown, white) in "What colour is…?" and "What's yellow/red…?" frames, big/small with contrast questions, water/window/watch and yak word sets, and the full capital alphabet A–Z ("These are big letters") with a join-big-and-small-letters exercise (only selected letters: n/h, s/H, y/w, etc.).
- Unit 8 Doing things (pp.51–56): present continuous — "I'm running / jumping / carrying a pot…", "What are they doing? Raju is sitting…", "What am I doing? You're dancing", "What's he/she doing?", "Are you dancing, Lila? — Yes, I am / No, I'm not", "Who's reading? Imran is", and choose-the-correct-sentence writing (dancing/standing/sitting etc.).
- Unit 9 Age and days (pp.57–61): "How old are you? — I'm five/six", "How old is he/Raju?", a follow-the-string age-writing exercise (Gita, Hari, Bina, Pemba, Lok, Rita, Anup), days of the week Sunday–Saturday with the chant noting Saturday is the holiday (the Nepali school week), "What day is it today?", flashcard ordering, gap-fill day spellings (Su_day, Tu_sd_y…), and a mixed review choose-words-from-the-bottom page.
- Unit 10 My family and things (pp.62–66): family chant (father, mother, sister, brother), "What's your father's/sister's name?", have got ("I've got a book/a ball", "What have you got, Numa?", "How many sisters/goats/cows have you got?"), a short reading text about Lilu's house and family, and two reading passages — a where-is-the-cat search (under the table/on the roof/in the pot/under the mat/in the box) and the story of Maya's lost sock, found on her own foot, with her doll Sita.
- Word list (pp.67–68): a two-page alphabetical vocabulary list of the book's words (a/an to yellow), including verb forms with -ing variants.

### Grammar spine
No grammar is named or explained; points arrive as spoken/written frames in this order: greetings and I'm… introductions → she's/he's for third-person naming → it's a… naming → imperatives (stand up, point to, touch) with please-requests → this/that → an before vowel-initial nouns inside "It's an orange/apple/elephant/egg" → my/his/her possessive adjectives → yes/no questions with is ("Is this an ant? — Yes, it is / No, it isn't") → this/these, that/those plurals → there is/there are with how many → where's…? with on/in/under → possessives my/your and his/her contrasted → colour and size adjectives in what's/what colour frames → present continuous in all persons (I'm, you're, he's/she's, they) with what…doing? and who's…? questions and short answers → how old…? with I'm five → have got ("I've got…", "How many … have you got?"). No past tense, no future form, and no do/does questions appear anywhere.

### Honest negatives
- No phonics system and no letter names taught: letters appear only as initial sounds of pictured word sets and as shapes to copy in four-line copy; the full alphabet appears once, as capitals to copy and match (p.50), with no lowercase alphabet chart and no sounds-for-letters table.
- No stories in the story sense until Unit 10's two short reading passages; there are no poems beyond chants/rhymes, no dialogues longer than a few exchanges, and no listening texts section (listening is teacher-read prompts marked only by the Nepali teacher notes).
- No numbers above ten as a topic (numbers to ten in Unit 5/7 frames only; no teens, no tens, no arithmetic), no months, no time-telling, no weather, no animals topic beyond incidental words (hen, goat, cat, dog, elephant, snake, rat).
- No tests, revision units or answer keys in the pupil's book; writing never goes beyond copying, completing single frames, and one-word gap fills.

### Slips and errors as printed
- Contents (p.iii of the PDF, Contents page) shortens Unit 3 to "Giving commands" where the unit's own heading (p.15) is "Giving commands and making requests" — contents-vs-book wording difference.
- Preface (PDF p.3) is signed for the "Ministry of Education and Sports" while the cover and imprint (PDF pp.1–2) say "Ministry of Education" — inconsistent ministry name inside one book.
- Preface (PDF p.3): "This book, originally written by Ranjana Bajagain and revised by … in 2003." — the sentence has no main verb for "This book" (a "was" is missing).
- Word list (p.67) prints "big" twice in the b-column and lists "black" and "blackboard" in an order that breaks the column's alphabetisation; the list also carries "class/room" as one entry.
- Unit 9 (p.61), the review page's word bank prints "on ............... table" and "big bus" run together with the other bank words (standing, pot, my, his, are you) in a way that makes the bank hard to parse — layout slip, not a language error.

### Copy completeness and cross-check
- Folio verdict: complete. Printed folios run 1–68 unbroken on PDF pp.5–72; no missing or duplicated leaves; the file ends on the Word list's second page (p.68) and has no back cover.
- Contents vs actual units: all 10 contents entries match the unit openings in number and page (Starting school 1, Sounds and letter shapes 7, Giving commands 15, Talking together 22, One and many 29, Where things are 36, Numbers and colours 44, Doing things 51, Age and days 57, My family and things 62); the only wording difference is the Unit 3 shortening noted above. The Word list (pp.67–68) is not listed in the Contents.
- Cross-check vs the recorded companion, Teacher's Guide My English Grade 1 (g01-teacher.pdf): the guide does NOT belong to this book. The guide is the First Edition 2020 guide to the new integrated-curriculum workbook 'My English Book One' (108 lessons in 11 themes: Me and My Family, Alphabet, My Daily Life, Our School, Our Environment, My Belongings, Our Culture, Communication/Technology/Market, Fruits and Vegetables, Hobbies and Interests, Birds and Animals), published by the Ministry of Education, Science and Technology. It contains no reference to this book's 10 units, its characters' exercises, or its page numbers; unit order, contents and keys therefore cannot agree — there is nothing in the guide that keys to this student's book. The pairing in the record is a generation mismatch (2003/2011 textbook with a 2020 guide), not a companion pair. See the guide's own filing for its full contents.

## Grade 1 — My English 1 Teacher's Guide (teacher's guide)

### Identity block
- Title as printed: title page (PDF p.2) "My English / 1 / Teacher's Guide"; running footer "Teacher's Guide : My English Book 1". The record calls it "Teacher's Guide My English Grade 1" — the printed title names the companion workbook 'My English Book One', and that wording matters: this guide belongs to the new integrated-curriculum Grade 1 workbook, NOT to the held Grade 1 student's book (the 2003 book, reprint 2011, filed as bandA-g01-student.md). PDF p.1 is the cover (a scanned image with no text layer).
- Publisher: Government of Nepal, Ministry of Education, Science and Technology, Curriculum Development Centre, Sanothimi, Nepal (title page); imprint (PDF p.3) "Publisher: Ministry of Education, Science and Technology / Curriculum Development Centre / Sanothimi, Bhaktapur", "© Publisher".
- Year / edition / ISBN as printed: "First Edition: 2020" (imprint). Computer layout by Khados Sunuwar; the "Printed by:" line is left blank. No ISBN is printed anywhere in the file; no price is printed.
- Developers/authors as printed: the Preface (PDF p.4, dated 2020) names the preparing team — Dr. Ganga Ram Gautam, Mr. Shankar Adhikari, Mr. Nim Prakash Singh Rathaur, Mr. Nabin Kumar Khadka and Mr. Ramesh Dhakal — with guidance and feedback from the Director General of the CDC, Mr. Keshav Prasad Dahal, and contributions from the subject committee chair Prof. Dr. Balmukunda Bhandari and other members.
- Curriculum/series notes: guide to the integrated-curriculum Grade 1 English workbook 'My English Book One' — 108 lessons in 11 thematic sections (one theme, Me and My Family, occurs twice), organised in three phases: Foundation phase (Lessons 1–14), Decodable phase (Lessons 15–72, phonics), Post-decodable phase (Lessons 73–108, fluency and comprehension). Every theme ends with an 'Assess Yourself' section plus suggested continuous-assessment tasks.
- Extent: 458 PDF pages = cover (PDF 1) + title page (PDF 2) + imprint (PDF 3) + Preface (PDF 4) + Table of Contents (PDF 5–6) + printed pp.1–452 (PDF 7–458).
- Printed↔PDF offset: printed = PDF − 6. Established from the Table of Contents (About the Teacher's Book at printed pp.1–49, starting PDF 7) and the printed folios, which match this offset throughout (e.g. printed p.50 at PDF 56; printed p.452 at PDF 458, the last page).

### Unit-by-unit summary
The guide's own structure replaces "units" with themes and numbered lessons; each lesson plan gives a lesson-focus table (skill-wise objectives, key words, text types), timed topics/activities with step-by-step procedures, and homework. Summary by section, with printed page ranges:
- About the Teacher's Guide (pp.1–49): how the workbook is built (three phases; headings like look and read, listen and sing, read and write; skill icons), principles of learning facilitation, basic considerations for classroom activities (study of curriculum, multiple senses, soft skills, communicative tasks, correct language model, self-pacing, inclusiveness, assessment as/for learning, remedial teaching, grade teaching), the "I do, we do and you do" sequence worked through a drilling example, and a large bank of suggested activities by stage and skill — foundation-stage listening/speaking (sound identification, animal and object sounds, picture discussion), pre-writing (colouring shapes, tracing, palm/finger writing), phonological-awareness games (rhyming bingo, sound-talk, marching around the alphabet, memory match), blending/segmenting activities, writing-readiness (letter-formation stages, missing-letter and unscramble tasks), following instructions, vocabulary race/anagram, and reading techniques (model reading, sentence substitution, picture walks, paired reading, repeated reading).
- Me and My Family — Foundation phase, Lessons 1–14 (pp.50–96): pre-reading/pre-writing lessons built on greetings and family — good morning/goodbye rhymes and greetings practice (Lessons 1–5), introducing yourself by name ("My name is…", "I am…", Lessons 6–7), family members father/mother/brother/sister/grandfather/grandmother with family photos and rhymes (Lessons 8–11), and animal sounds and animals-in-action picture work (Lessons 12–14); every lesson repeats the same four-topic pattern — listen and say/act, point/colour/tick the same picture, trace the way/join the dots, listen and sing — with colouring, tracing and line-drawing as the writing work. Ends with an 'Assess Yourself' section for the theme.
- Alphabet — Decodable phase, Lessons 15–72 (pp.97–278): 58 phonics lessons. Lessons 15–25 cover Aa–Ee, 26–36 Ff–Jj, 37–47 Kk–Oo, and the sequence continues letter by letter through Pp (48–50), Qq (50–52 in the lesson-focus tables; lessons 50–52), Rr (52–53), Ss (54–55), Tt (56–57), Uu (59–60), Vv (61–62), Ww (63–64), Xx (65–66), Yy (67–68), Zz (69–70), with blending checkpoints at Lessons 25, 36, 47, 58 and review lessons 71–72 (Aa–Zz). Each letter gets the same two-lesson treatment: lesson A introduces the letter and its sound with picture words, look-and-discuss, listen-and-say, matching words that begin with the letter, colour, and four-line writing of the letter; lesson B adds reading (chain reading of decodable words), look-and-say, copy/write, and homework ("Write the letters 'B' and 'b' with the help of your seniors"). Worked examples in the tables include apple/ant/axe/arrow/aeroplane/aunt for A and eel/bee/bed for E (with 'e' also taught inside decodable words).
- Me and My Family (second occurrence) — Lessons 73–74 (pp.279–287): the post-decodable phase opens under the family theme heading but the two lessons are body lessons — Lesson 73 Head and Shoulders (body parts, look and read, sing and act) and Lesson 74 I See with My Eyes (sense organs — eye, nose, ear, tongue, skin — read and write, homework "Write the functions of the five sense organs").
- My Daily Life — Lessons 75–79 (pp.288–314): Lesson 75 Brush Your Teeth (daily hygiene imperatives: wash your face, brush your teeth, comb your hair, cut your nail; a where-is-it question game with cat, bee, pond, ground, leaf, ant, fly); Lesson 76 I Can Jump (can/can't for ability with draw, swim, read, write; monkey dialogue); Lesson 77 I'm Happy (feelings — happy, sad, cry; the Sunita text and its questions); Lesson 78 It's Seven O'clock (telling the time by hours with a hand-moved clock); Lesson 79 Seven Days (days of the week in order; yesterday, today and tomorrow).
- Our School — Lessons 80–84 (pp.315–339): Lesson 80 Come Here (instructions and present-action talk), Lesson 81 May I…? (asking permission — "May I…", "Yes, you can", "No, you can't", with rising-tone practice), Lesson 82 I'm Pawan (introducing a person — himself/herself, class, year), Lesson 83 Things in the Classroom (table, bench, dustbin, rack; where-are-the-chairs questions), Lesson 84 Let's Play (games and skipping). Ends with 'Assess Yourself 4' and continuous-assessment tasks for My School.
- Our Environment — Lessons 85–88 (pp.340–357): Lesson 85 On the Table (location with on — mat, book, roof, table, cup), Lesson 86 The Rainbow (colours — black, green, blue, yellow — and rainbow), Lesson 87 Red Rose (flowers and colours — marigold/Sayapatri yellow, Makhamali red), Lesson 88 This is a Bag (this/that for pointing out things — "That is a tree. That is a car. That is an apple").
- My Belongings — Lessons 89–92 (pp.358–374): Lesson 89 In the Bag (in — cage, box; classroom/bucket/pot situations), Lesson 90 Under the Table (under, with gloves and helmet among the words), Lesson 91 Things at Home (chair, television, sofa, fan; school things — book, exercise book, sharpner [sic]), Lesson 92 Where Are My Socks? (clothes — shirt, frock, tie, socks, ribbon, jacket; the contents list misprints the title as "Where Are My Shocks?", see Slips).
- Our Culture — Lessons 93–94 (pp.375–385): Lesson 93 Dashain (festivals — Dashain, Christmas, Lhosar, Chhat; Hindus, Buddhists, Muslims, Christians; unscrambling words to make sentences), Lesson 94 Birthday (birthday vocabulary — cake, candle, cup, gifts; numbers one to twenty).
- Communication, Technology and Market — Lessons 95–97 (pp.386–399): Lesson 95 Ramu Is a Farmer (occupations — farmer, porter, doctor, driver, with a who-does-what matching: a doctor works in a hospital, a driver drives a car), Lesson 96 Rice and Curry (food — rice, curry, water, egg, meat; dictation with self- and peer-correction), Lesson 97 Vehicles (bicycle, tempo and other vehicles).
- Fruits and Vegetables — Lessons 98–100 (pp.400–414): Lesson 98 Fruits (coconut, grapes, banana, papaya), Lesson 99 A/An (the guide's one explicit grammar explanation: "We use 'a' with a noun starting with consonant letter (sound) and 'an' with a noun starting with vowel letter (sound)"; writing sentences about an apple tree, a boy, a girl), Lesson 100 Vegetables (onion, pumpkin, cauliflower, cucumber).
- Hobbies and Interests — Lessons 101–102 (pp.415–424): Lesson 101 I Like Fruits (likes — read, dance, draw), Lesson 102 I Don't Like Leaves (likes and dislikes — sing, dance, play, paint, draw; "What do you like to do? What don't you like to do?").
- Birds and Animals — Lessons 103–108 (pp.425–452): Lesson 103 The Tiger (wild animals — monkey, jackal; "It is a tiger. I find it in the jungle"), Lesson 104 The Cock (birds — pigeon and others; colour the name of the bird), Lesson 105 Birds in the Tree (peacock; dictation), Lesson 106 On/In/Under (the rabbit; hole, cat, mat, carrot; please/welcome/thanks), Lesson 107 Big and Small (adjectives), Lesson 108 My Pet (pet, guards; the closing sample text "The boy has a dog. He has a cat. He loves them"). The book ends at printed p.452 with Lesson 108's writing activity.

### Grammar spine (as the guide frames it)
The guide teaches almost no grammar as named topics — language points sit inside lesson-focus tables as "language function" items — but its sequence is: greetings and self/family naming frames → the full phonics spine (letter sounds in alphabetical order, blending and segmenting, decodable words, rhyming) → body-part naming and sense verbs (I see with my eyes) → hygiene imperatives → can/can't for ability → feelings adjectives → clock time (o'clock) and days with yesterday/today/tomorrow → classroom instructions → may I…? permission with yes you can/no you can't → himself/herself in introductions → this/that (Lesson 88) → prepositions on (85), in (89), under (90) gathered up as on/in/under (106) → festival and occupation naming → a/an as the single explicitly explained rule (Lesson 99) → like/don't like with verb phrases (101–102) → adjectives big/small (107). No tense system is taught: past and future forms occur only inside model texts and never as points; no plurals, possessives, comparatives or question-formation teaching is framed anywhere in the guide.

### Honest negatives
- The guide contains nothing at all about the held Grade 1 student's book: no mention of its 10 units (Starting school … My family and things), its characters' exercises, its page numbers, its word list, or its Nepali teacher notes — checked across the contents and the full lesson sequence, not assumed.
- No answer keys in the modern sense: the guide gives procedures, sample answers and model sentences, but it does not print a key to the workbook's exercises page by page.
- No ISBN, no price, no printer (the "Printed by:" line is blank), no edition beyond "First Edition: 2020", and no statement of how the guide relates to any student's book other than naming 'My English Book One'.
- No grammar reference section, no word list, no audio/listening scripts section (listening is teacher-modelled throughout), and no provision for the old book's four-line copy system beyond letter-writing inside the Alphabet lessons.

### Slips and errors as printed
- Table of Contents (PDF p.5): "Lesson 92: Where Are My Shocks?" — "Shocks" for "Socks"; the lesson itself (printed p.372) is headed "Where are my Socks?" and its words are socks, ribbon and other clothes.
- Table of Contents (PDF pp.5–6) lists the theme "Me and My Family" twice — at pp.50–96 for Lessons 1–14 and again at pp.279–287 for Lessons 73–74 — but Lessons 73–74 are body/senses lessons (Head and Shoulders; I See with My Eyes), so the repeated theme heading does not describe them.
- Contents (PDF p.6) calls Lesson 88 "This is a Bag" while the Our Environment theme page (printed p.340) lists it as "There is a Bag"; the lesson's own work is this/that pointing ("That is a tree…").
- Lesson 99's lesson-focus table (printed p.406) repeats Lesson 98's table — the same key words (coconut, grapes, banana, papaya) and "text for comprehension" — although Lesson 99 is the a/an lesson; the a/an explanation itself appears only inside the activity text (printed p.408).
- Printed p.290 area (Lesson 75): the theme page (printed p.288) lists Lesson 76 as "I can Jump" with lower-case "can" against the title-case used for the other lessons.
- Printed p.372 (Lesson 91's materials list): "book, exercise book, sharpner" — "sharpner" for "sharpener".
- Printed p.400 (Fruits and Vegetables theme page): "Vegetbles (Lesson 100)" — "Vegetbles" for "Vegetables".
- Printed p.451 (Lesson 108): "ask them to read the textone by one" — missing space ("text one by one").
- Printed p.2 (About the Teacher's Guide): "students cannot read the words and expressions in English here here but they can imitate the words" — duplicated "here".

### Copy completeness and cross-check
- Folio verdict: complete. Printed folios run 1–452 unbroken on PDF pp.7–458; no missing or duplicated leaves were found in the page-by-page pass; the file ends with Lesson 108's final activity on p.452.
- Contents vs actual: the Table of Contents' theme ranges and lesson page references agree with the lesson openings throughout (About 1–49; Me and My Family 50–96; Alphabet 97–278; Lessons 73–74 at 279; My Daily Life 288–314; Our School 315–339; Our Environment 340–357; My Belongings 358–374; Our Culture 375–385; Communication, Technology and Market 386–399; Fruits and Vegetables 400–414; Hobbies and Interests 415–424; Birds and Animals 425–452), with the Lesson 88/92 title slips noted above.
- Cross-check vs the recorded companion student's book (g01-student.pdf): TOTAL DISAGREEMENT — this is a record correction. The guide (First Edition 2020, Ministry of Education, Science and Technology, integrated curriculum) keys to a workbook that is not held: 'My English Book One' with 108 lessons. The held student's book is the 2003 CDC textbook (reprint 2011, Ministry of Education) with 10 units and 68 printed pages. No unit, lesson, character exercise or page reference is shared between the two files; neither book mentions the other. The two files are a mismatched pair from two different curriculum generations, and any filing that treats this guide as the companion/answer key to the held Grade 1 student's book would be wrong.

## Grade 2 — MY ENGLISH BOOK Grade II (student's book)

### Identity block
- Title as printed: cover "MY ENGLISH BOOK / Grade II"; the running footer throughout is "MY ENGLISH BOOK, Grade 2". The record calls it "My English Book Grade 2" — the printed cover grade form is the Roman numeral "Grade II" while the footer uses the Arabic "Grade 2"; there is no separate title page (PDF p.1 is the cover, PDF p.2 is the imprint page).
- Publisher: Government of Nepal, Ministry of Education, Curriculum Development Centre, Sanothimi, Bhaktapur, NEPAL (cover and imprint, PDF pp.1–2).
- Year / edition / ISBN as printed: ISBN 978-999-33-708-7-8 (imprint, printed as "ISBN: 978-999-33-708-7-8"). "First edition: 2004 / Second edition: 2006 / Reprint: 2011". The imprint carries the CDC approval note for printing/distribution by the private sector in 32 districts of the Eastern and Western Development Region for academic year 2068 BS under the 'School Textbook Printing and Distribution Directives - 2067 BS', a blank printer/price block, and the printer's note "If you find any printing error in your book, you can exchange from the local sales agent."
- Developers/authors as printed: no cover authors. The Preface (PDF p.3) states the book was "originally written by Pramod Kumar Shah, Krishna Raj Hamal and Dev Narayan Mukhiya" and "edited by the Curriculum Development Centre"; layout design by Navindra Rajbhandari and Himalaya Gautam; illustrations by Abindra Man Shrestha.
- Curriculum/series notes: second book of the CDC primary English series that began with English from Grade One in 2003 (Preface). Like Grade 1, it interleaves short teacher directions in Nepali on many pages (legible on the page; the embedded font extracts as mojibake). Units are headed "Unit One… Unit Twelve" in words.
- Extent: 80 PDF pages = cover (PDF 1) + imprint (PDF 2) + Preface (PDF 3) + Contents (PDF 4) + printed pp.1–76 (PDF 5–80). Printed p.76 is the last Word list page; there is no back cover in the file.
- Printed↔PDF offset: printed = PDF − 4. Established from the Contents (Unit 1 at printed p.1 = PDF 5; Listening texts 74; Word List 75) and the printed folios, which match this offset throughout to p.76 (PDF 80).

### Unit-by-unit summary
- Unit One Going to school (pp.1–7): greetings revisited and extended — good morning/afternoon/evening, good night, goodbye/bye bye, "How are you? I'm fine. How about you? I'm also fine", introductions ("Hello! I'm Mr. Gurung. What is your name? My name is Pukar"), please sit down/thank you Sir, point-and-say naming (cat, fish, mango, dog, apple, flower), "What is this? It's a clock", a first-letter completion exercise teaching that a person's name begins with a capital letter, an oral conversation (going to market; "See you"), "See you tomorrow", and a listen-and-repeat on the ee sound (sheep, bean, seat, feet, cheeks).
- Unit Two What am I? (pp.8–12): occupations and identity — "Who is she? She is my friend, Preeti. What is she? She is a student", "What's his name? … Is he a soldier? No, he isn't. What is he? He is a farmer", what's-that-in-his-hand (spade, duster), match-and-say occupations (farmer, doctor, student, policeman, shopkeeper, teacher), an occupations chant, letter completion, and an oral conversation "What's your father? My father is a doctor?" (sic — see Slips).
- Unit Three Numbers (pp.13–19): how many…? there are… with counts to twelve and beyond, a family-and-ages reading (grandfather 70, grandmother 65, father 35, mother 30, Pukar 6, uncle 30, aunt 25, brother and sister 8 and 9), number-word matching 11–50 (eleven, twenty, twenty-five, thirty-three, thirty-five, thirty-six, forty-one, forty-seven, fifty), a zoo chant, number-word spelling gap fills (fifty, seventeen, nineteen, forty, forty-three…), counting pictured objects (birds, spiders, bicycles, padlocks, dogs), writing numbers in words up to 97 (78, 93, 62, 97, 56, 69, 68, 81, 89, 70, 86), an oral conversation with "How many books do you have? I have ten books", and listen-and-repeat on look/pool/boot/pull/foot/full.
- Unit Four Commands (pp.20–25): imperatives and negative imperatives — open the door, go out, sit down against don't jump, don't sit down, don't eat that food; verb matching (drink, dance, laugh, drive, clap, jump); a "Ride it, ride it, but don't fall" chant; don't touch the fire, don't talk, keep quiet; letter completion teaching that a sentence begins with a capital letter; listen-and-repeat pairs (long/wrong, light/right, load/road); and a listen-and-write dialogue exercise keyed to the Listening text on p.74.
- Unit Five What have you got? (pp.26–32): have got/has got in all persons — "Have you got a pen, Nima? Yes, I have / No, I haven't", "How many books have you got, Pukar? I have five books", have/has with sisters, brother, Nima, Preeti (has got a small radio, a big bag; hasn't got a crayon), match-and-say (a doll, a pair of shoes, a hat, a piece of bone, a big bag), the "Billy Boy" chant (which answers "Have you got a pen?" once with "No, I don't" — see Slips), "What has Preeti got? She has got a story book", silent-k listen-and-repeat (knee, knot, knit, knife), and read-and-write has-got sentences.
- Unit Six What someone is doing? (pp.33–38): present continuous extended — "What are you doing? I'm reading a book", "What is he wearing? He's wearing a pair of socks", "What is she eating? She's eating a banana", is he/she…ing? corrections ("Is Pukar swimming in the river? No, he's swimming in the pool"), action matching (running, singing, playing, weeping, riding, looking, punching), where-is/what-doing pairs with in the pool / playing badminton / under / over, an oral conversation (going to the book-shop, carrying books), read-and-draw, is/are gap fills with punctuation, and listen-and-repeat (read, beat, eat, bean, clean).
- Unit Seven Where things are? (pp.39–45): prepositions extended beyond Grade 1's on/in/under — next to, in front of, behind, on top of, inside, outside, near — in where-is frames, a read-and-say puppy hunt (under the table, outside the house, on top of the bus, near the tree), a preposition chant ("A dog sleeps, on the table…"), look-and-write, listen-and-do (put your books in your bag), and an oral conversation (dog behind the house barking; a cat under the table; a cricket bat on the table).
- Unit Eight People and things (pp.46–50): adjectives and mine/yours — "Which one is my doll? That one. Your doll is small. Mine is big", describing with tall/short, fat/thin, beautiful/ugly, an opposites matching exercise (tall–short, small–big, wide–narrow, beautiful–ugly, black–white, thin–fat, near–far), adjective gap fills, and "Which shirt is new? This one…" identification questions (with one mismatched answer — see Slips).
- Unit Nine Describing people (pp.51–55): a description frame (name, age, hair colour, fat or thin, occupation), two model descriptions with comprehension questions — Bir Bahadur (50, white shirt, tall and thin, long nose, reading a newspaper, teacher) and Bindu (30, glasses and white sari, fat, works in hospital, nurse) — a picture-description writing task (Kamala), look-and-say description questions, the "Butterfly, Butterfly" chant, a preposition completion review, and listen-and-repeat (chalk, walk, talk).
- Unit Ten Time and days (pp.56–61): "What's the time? It's 10 o'clock", half past ("It's half past four"), what-are-you-doing at a time, today/yesterday/tomorrow with days of the week, an oral conversation on routines with third-person -s ("What time does your sister get up? She gets up at 6:30… She goes to bed at 8:30"), "What day is today? It's Friday today… Yesterday was Thursday", a birthday dialogue ("Today is Monday. It's my birthday. Happy birthday, Mohan"), putting the days in order, and a born-on-a-day game.
- Unit Eleven Past action (pp.62–66): past continuous and past simple introduced together — "Where was Nima this morning? She was at home… What was she doing? She was doing her homework", was/were dialogues (library/playground, reading a newspaper, riding a bicycle), "What did Prashant do yesterday? He went to the zoo", past-tense action answers (flew a kite, wrote a letter, watched TV, cleaned the window, went to market, drew a picture, phoned my friend), a "Swifty sat on the flower" chant, and write-sentences with flew/phoned/played/went/wrote.
- Unit Twelve Good friends (pp.67–73): a picture story told in captions — a boy going to the city is warned by a bird that a tiger is coming, climbs a tree, and in return warns the bird when a hunter points a gun at it ("Run away, my friend"); word matching (bird, tiger, tree, hunter, climb up, run away, gun); past-tense question work on the story (who went/helped/saw, what did the boy do) with a choose-the-word cloze (helped, said, pointed, climbed, saw, went, was); look-and-order yesterday-morning pictures; a routine-recall dialogue ("What time did Madan get up? He got up at 6… He brushed his teeth… He drank tea"); matching write-sentences (read, went, drank, got up, ate, brushed); and the "Mote Dai" chant (went to the jungle, saw a tiger, a lion, a monkey and an elephant).
- Listening texts (p.74): the teacher-read scripts for Unit 4 (five don't-commands), Unit 8 (five adjective sentences) and Unit 11 (Pukar sleeping/drinking tea/playing with his dog Kalu).
- Word List (pp.75–76): alphabetical vocabulary list for the book (about/aeroplane to zoo), reusing the Grade 1 list format.

### Grammar spine
Frames again, never named rules — the sequence is: greetings/introductions extended (how about you, see you tomorrow) → who/what-is-she questions for identity and occupation with he/she and his/her → how many…? there are… with number words to 50 and written numbers to 97 → imperatives and don't-imperatives → have got/has got with have/has questions and haven't/hasn't negatives for all persons → present continuous extended (wearing, eating; is/are questions with correction answers) → the full preposition set (next to, in front of, behind, on top of, inside, outside, near) → adjectives with opposites and possessive pronouns mine/yours (adjectives only; his/hers not used as pronouns) → description frames with is wearing and works in → clock time (o'clock, half past) and present-simple routines with third-person -s in questions and answers (does your sister get up / she gets up, goes) — the book's only do/does teaching → past continuous (was/were …ing) and past simple (did…? went, flew, wrote, drank, brushed, ate, got up) introduced side by side in Unit 11 and practised through the Unit 12 story. No future form, no comparative forms (big/small are adjectives only), no articles teaching beyond use, no passive, no reported speech.

### Honest negatives
- No phonics programme: sounds appear only as short listen-and-repeat word sets at unit ends (ee, oo, kn-, ea, ch/alk etc.), with no blending, no letter-sound table and no alphabet revision.
- No months of the year, no dates, no seasons/weather topic, no money, no food topic beyond incidental words — several Grade 1 topics (colours) are reused but not retaught as a unit.
- Only one full story (Unit 12's Good friends, told in picture captions) plus the two short model descriptions in Unit 9; no poems beyond chants, no letters, no longer reading texts.
- No comparative/superlative grammar, no future forms (will/going to absent), no can/can't (Grade 1's frames are not continued), no must/should, no possessive pronouns beyond mine/yours, no object pronouns as a topic.
- No tests or revision units inside the book (Unit 11/12 recycle earlier frames, and p.74 holds the only listening scripts — for Units 4, 8 and 11 only); no answer key for pupils.

### Slips and errors as printed
- p.7 (Unit 1, ex.4 rubric): "Copy in you exercise book." — "you" for "your".
- p.12 (Unit 2 oral conversation): "Nima: My father is a doctor?" — a statement printed with a question mark.
- p.17 (Unit 3, ex.7): "How may bicycles are there?" — "may" for "many".
- p.26 (Unit 5 chant): the "Billy Boy" chant answers "Have you got a pen?" with "No, I don't, no, I don't" — the book's own frame everywhere else is "No, I haven't"; the chant contradicts the unit's target pattern.
- p.50 (Unit 8, ex.6): "Which coat is clean? — This one. This is a clean shirt." — question asks about a coat, answer names a shirt.
- p.52 (Unit 9, Bindu text): "She works in hospital." — missing article ("in a hospital" / "in the hospital").
- p.70 (Unit 12, ex.4 rubric): "Ask an answer." — for "Ask and answer".
- Word list (p.75) prints "falm" (for "palm"? — no palm is taught in the book), "knite" alongside "knot" and "knife" (no "knite" is taught), and "grand mother" as two words; the same list carries entries (falm, knite) that repeat faults of the Grade 1 list's tradition.
- Contents (PDF p.4) prints unit titles with question marks — "Unit 6 What someone is doing?" and "Unit 7 Where things are?" — and the unit headings themselves (pp.33, 39) keep the statement-with-question-mark form.

### Copy completeness and cross-check
- Folio verdict: complete. Printed folios run 1–76 unbroken on PDF pp.5–80; no missing or duplicated leaves; the file ends on the Word List's second page (p.76) and has no back cover.
- Contents vs actual units: all 12 contents entries match the unit openings in number, title and page (Going to school 1, What am I? 8, Numbers 13, Commands 20, What have you got? 26, What someone is doing? 33, Where things are? 39, People and things 46, Describing people 51, Time and days 56, Past action 62, Good friends 67), and the Listening texts (74) and Word List (75) entries also match.
- Cross-check vs the recorded companion, Teacher's Guide English Grade Two (g02-teacher.pdf): the guide does NOT belong to this book. The guide is the "Frist edition: 2021" guide to the new integrated-curriculum Grade 2 workbook (44 lessons in 10 themes — Me and My Family, My Daily Life, My School, Our Environment, My Belongings, Our Culture, Communication Technology and Market, Fruits and Vegetables, Hobbies and Interests, Birds and Animals — with Lessons 41–44 on Chicken Little, Helpful Animals, Birds and Animals and What do Animals Eat?), published by the Ministry of Education, Science and Technology under the curriculum introduced in grades 1–3 from year 2077 BS. It contains no reference to this book's 12 units, its Pukar/Nima/Preeti exercises, or its page numbers; unit order, contents and keys cannot agree — nothing in the guide keys to this student's book. The pairing in the record is a generation mismatch (2004/2011 textbook with a 2021 guide), not a companion pair. See the guide's own filing for its full contents.

## Grade 2 — TEACHER'S GUIDE ENGLISH GRADE TWO (teacher's guide)

### Identity block
- Title as printed: title page (PDF p.2) "TEACHER'S GUIDE / ENGLISH GRADE TWO"; running footer "Teacher's Guide: English Grade Two". The record calls it "Teacher's Guide My English Book Grade 2" — the printed title does not contain the words "My English Book"; the guide keys to the new integrated-curriculum "book English Grade 2" (Preface), NOT to the held Grade 2 student's book (the 2004 book, second edition 2006, reprint 2011, filed as bandA-g02-student.md). PDF p.1 is the cover (a scanned image with no text layer).
- Publisher: Government of Nepal, Ministry of Education, Science and Technology, Curriculum Development Centre, Sanothimi, Bhaktapur (title page and imprint, PDF pp.2–3); imprint "© Curriculum Development Centre".
- Year / edition / ISBN as printed: the imprint (PDF p.3) reads "Frist edition: 2021" [sic — see Slips]. The Preface is dated "June, 2021". No ISBN is printed anywhere in the file; no price and no printer are printed.
- Developers/authors as printed: the Preface (PDF p.4) names the writing team — Shankar Adhikari, Nim Prakash Singh Rathour, Nabin Kumar Khadka and Ramesh Dhakal — with each member's themes listed (Adhikari: My Belongings, Hobbies and Interests, Birds and Animals; Rathour: My Daily Life and My School; Khadka: Our Environment, Communication Technology and Market; Dhakal: Me and My Family, Our Culture, Fruits and Vegetables), input from Ana Prasad Neupane, Prof. Dr. Jib Lal Sapkota, Prof. Dr. Rish Ram Rijal, Dr. Gopal Prasad Pandey, Maiya Pokharel, Madhav Prasad Ghimire and Tuka Raj Adhikari, and editing by Mr. Birat Chaulagain.
- Curriculum/series notes: guide to the integrated curriculum introduced in grades 1–3 from the year 2077 BS (Preface): English is one of four major learning areas, organised in 10 themes, each theme ending with an assessment section (portfolio files and scoring sheets; assessment is done twice — the workbook tasks first, then teacher-designed tasks after a remedial class). Each activity of the workbook is reproduced ("snipped") on the right side of the guide's procedure pages. Lessons are explicitly "not equal to one period"; theme time is distributed among activities.
- Extent: 187 PDF pages = cover (PDF 1) + title page (PDF 2) + imprint (PDF 3) + Preface (PDF 4) + Table of Contents (PDF 5–6) + blank page (PDF 7) + printed pp.1–180 (PDF 8–187).
- Printed↔PDF offset: printed = PDF − 7. Established from the Table of Contents (Introduction at printed pp.1–5, starting PDF 8; Lesson 1 at printed p.7) and the printed folios, which match this offset to the end (printed p.179 at PDF 186; printed p.180 at PDF 187, the last page).

### Unit-by-unit summary
The guide is organised in 10 themes of 3–6 lessons each (44 lessons), every lesson following the same shape — theme/lesson heading, number of topics, stepwise timed procedures for each workbook activity with the skill it focuses on, answer notes where needed, integration ideas for the other subjects, and homework — and every theme closing with an Assessment section of five tasks (listening, dictation, pair speaking, read-aloud plus comprehension, and a writing task) scored 1–4 into each student's portfolio.
- Introduction (pp.1–5): what the guide is, how the workbook's skill icons work (listen, speak, read, write, match, listen and sing/say/act), what the instruction background colours mean, background example language, and 13 numbered teaching directions ending with "Use remedial class" (p.4 in the printed run).
- Theme: Me and My Family (theme pp.6–11; Lessons 1–6, pp.7–29; Assessment 1, p.30): Lesson 1 Birendra's Family (family members, reading with answers located in the text, a dictation of words like brother); Lesson 2 I Love My Family (family statements, true/false reading); Lesson 3 Uncle Shiva's Family (wider family relations); Lesson 4 It's Me (self — "I have a brother. I like my family", word learning, a make-your-profile task that can double as the Nepali outcome); Lesson 5 My Body (body parts — "This is my mouth"); Lesson 6 I Take Care of My Body (healthy food, rest, games and exercise). Assessment 1's note corrects a name in the workbook ("There should be 'Preeti' in the questions").
- Theme: My Daily Life (pp.32–54; Lessons 7–12; Assessment 2, p.53): Lesson 7 Before School (morning activities and time), Lesson 8 At School (school activities, pair question-and-answer), Lesson 9 After School (evening activities, days), Lesson 10 On Saturdays (Saturday activities; picture sentences "A man is selling fruits. A girl is reading a book. A girl is watering flowers"), Lesson 11 At the Zoo (holiday animals — monkey, peacock, lion, lophophores [sic in the elicitation list], antelope, ostrich, crocodile, elephant, owls, bear, pigeon), Lesson 12 At a Fair. Assessment 2 includes an and/but gap-fill writing test.
- Theme: My School (pp.55–75; Lessons 13–18; Assessment 3, p.74): Lesson 13 Lunch Box Surprise (snacks; answers given — "Sandwich and carrot sticks"), Lesson 14 Dentist at School, Lesson 15 Homework (a Riyana text; answer "Reading"), Lesson 16 Durbar High School (a school, its parts and location; letter components — place "Kathmandu", date "25 August, 2019" — taught inside Lesson 21's guide pages but introduced here in the school sequence), Lesson 17 Unit Test, Lesson 18 We Are at School (the school timetable). Assessment 3's writing test is describing a picture in five sentences.
- Theme: Our Environment (pp.76–98; Lessons 19–24; Assessment 4, p.98): Lesson 19 My House (rooms — living room, bathroom, dining room — and present-continuous house sentences "Reena is cleaning the room. Subash is washing his hands…"), Lesson 20 My Garden (a garden description; "Ruby's teacher, Mr. Thapa is in her garden"), Lesson 21 My Village (villages; a letter's components), Lesson 22 Weather (a weather-report table; rewriting sentences as "It's sunny. It's rainy"), Lesson 23 Keeping Clean (wash, brush, sweep, bathe, clean; "I bathe every day. I brush my teeth twice a day"; do-you questions "Do you cut your nails? Yes, I do"), Lesson 24 Using Numbers (numbers 20–30 introduced with a Pass the Parcel game; listening number-names; rewriting a paragraph changing numbers into word form).
- Theme: My Belongings (pp.99–111; Lessons 25–27; Assessment 5, p.110): Lesson 25 Things I Have (possessions; the model text of Ram Karki — mobile phone, radio; yes/no questions), Lesson 26 Colours and Objects (colours; an answer set that runs ahead into a birthday text — "Laxmi's birthday is on March 24th. She is six years old"), Lesson 27 My Clothes. Assessment 5's vocabulary test: write as many words as possible from the letters of "belonging".
- Theme: Our Culture (pp.112–126; Lessons 28–30; Assessment 6, p.125): Lesson 28 Our Festivals (celebrations; writing five sentences about a favourite festival), Lesson 29 Children's Day, Lesson 30 Months of the Year (months in order; rearranging letters to make month names). Assessment 6's printed answers begin "Eight years old / Tina's family".
- Theme: Communication Technology and Market (pp.127–142; Lessons 31–34; Assessment 7, p.141): Lesson 31 Shopping (a shopping song/poem with European/Asian tourist and coin vocabulary — guess, coins, shiny, collect), Lesson 32 Talking on the Telephone (a shopping-centre dialogue worked question by question), Lesson 33 What's This (objects from a bag — watch, pencil, pen; shop drawing homework), Lesson 34 Making a List (book, bag, bottle, card, sandals, shoes, pants, shirt). Assessment 7 ends with unscramble-the-words.
- Theme: Fruits and Vegetables (pp.143–152; Lessons 35–37; Assessment 8, p.152): Lesson 35 Fruits at the Market (with the guide's own correction note: "There should be a picture of a jackfruit instead [of] the picture of litchi"), Lesson 36 Vegetables at the Market, Lesson 37 Buying Fruits and Vegetables ("Potatoes: I love to eat fried potatoes. Tomatoes: I like the pickle of tomatoes"; why people and animals eat fruits).
- Theme: Hobbies and Interests (pp.153–165; Lessons 38–40; Assessment 9, p.165): Lesson 38 I Like (likes; a sample free-time paragraph), Lesson 39 We Are on a Holiday (holiday activities and a Reena text; draw-and-write about your last holiday), Lesson 40 Playing Games ("A: Hello! Nabin. Let's play volleyball. B: Sure. Let's do. / No thanks."). 
- Theme: Birds and Animals (pp.166–180; Lessons 41–44; Assessment 10, pp.179–180): Lesson 41 Chicken Little (the acorn story — "What actually fell from the tree? (acorn)… Who went to the lion?"), Lesson 42 Helpful Animals (ending in a 30-minute paragraph task, "You must know something about the cow. Write a short paragraph about it"), Lesson 43 Birds and Animals (a dialogue about giving reasons; the procedure tells students to "read the questions on page 210" — a workbook page reference far beyond this guide's own pagination), Lesson 44 What Do Animals Eat? (a cat song; an MPF/MFP vocabulary model; reading a story sentence by sentence). Assessment 10's dictation sentences are: "The elephant is a very big animal", "The fish can swim but the bird can't", "Animals make sounds to talk to their friends", "Chicken Little met a duck on the way"; its last task is punctuating four sentences. The book ends at printed p.180.

### Grammar spine (as the guide frames it)
As with its Grade 1 counterpart, grammar is carried as language functions inside tasks, never as a syllabus of named topics; the sequence the procedures actually drill is: family/self naming with have ("I have a brother") → body-part this-is-my frames → daily routine present simple → present continuous for pictured activities (selling, reading, watering, cleaning, washing, dancing) → weather it's sunny/rainy frames → do you…? yes I do habits questions → numbers 20–30 and number words → possessions (things I have) and colours → months and dates → shopping and telephone dialogues → fruits/vegetables likes ("I love to eat…", "I like the pickle of…") → like-phrase hobbies and past-tense holiday recounts in writing ("What did you do on your last holiday?") → animal stories in the past (Chicken Little) with because-style reason-giving in Lesson 43. The only points the guide ever states as rules are punctuation (capital letters, full stops — drilled in every Assessment's "Punctuate the following" task) and, in passing, and/but as sentence joiners (Assessment 2). No tense is ever named; no comparative, passive, reported-speech or conditional work appears.

### Honest negatives
- The guide contains nothing at all about the held Grade 2 student's book: no mention of its 12 units (Going to school … Good friends), its Pukar/Nima/Preeti exercises, its listening texts or its page numbers — checked across the contents, the introduction and the full lesson sequence, not assumed.
- No ISBN, no price, no printer, and no edition statement beyond the misprinted "Frist edition: 2021"; no authors on the cover or title page (the team is named only in the Preface).
- No separate answer-key section: answers are scattered inside procedures (and occasionally run ahead of the lesson, e.g. Lesson 26's birthday answers), and several lessons give no answers at all.
- No word list, no listening scripts of its own (assessment listening texts are read by the teacher from the guide's dictation sentences), and no mapping from its 44 lessons to the old curriculum's Grade 2 scope.

### Slips and errors as printed
- Imprint (PDF p.3): "Frist edition: 2021" — "Frist" for "First", in the edition line itself.
- Lesson 43 procedure (printed p.174 area): "Tell the students to read the questions on page 210" — the guide itself has only 180 printed pages; the reference is to the companion workbook, whose pagination the guide nowhere explains.
- Lesson 35 note (printed p.144 area): "There should be a picture of a jackfruit instead the picture of litchi." — the guide corrects the workbook's picture but the sentence itself drops "of" after "instead".
- Lesson 11 elicitation list (printed p.48 area): "lophophores" among the zoo animals — a misspelt form of the national bird's name (lophophorus / danphe) printed in a vocabulary list teachers are told to elicit.
- Assessment 5 heading (printed p.110 area) prints the theme name with German-style low quotes: 'Theme „My Belongings"'.
- Lesson 27 procedure (printed p.108 area): "tell the the students to look at the picture" — duplicated "the".
- Preface vs Grade 1 guide: this Preface spells the team member "Nim Prakash Singh Rathour" where the Grade 1 guide's Preface (2020) spells the same man "Rathaur" — the two official guides disagree on their shared author's name (each is filed as printed).
- Introduction (printed p.1): "Here, lesson s are not equal to one period." — a stray space inside "lessons", in the paragraph that explains the guide's own structure.

### Copy completeness and cross-check
- Folio verdict: complete. Printed folios run 1–180 unbroken on PDF pp.8–187 (PDF 7 is a blank leaf between the contents and the Introduction); no missing or duplicated leaves were found in the page-by-page pass; the file ends with Assessment 10's closing direction on p.180.
- Contents vs actual: the Table of Contents' theme ranges and every lesson's page reference agree with the lesson openings throughout (Me and My Family 6–31, My Daily Life 32–54, My School 55–75, Our Environment 76–98, My Belongings 99–111, Our Culture 112–126, Communication Technology and Market 127–142, Fruits and Vegetables 143–152, Hobbies and Interests 153–165, Birds and Animals 166–180; Assessments 1–10 at pp.30, 53, 74, 98, 110, 125, 141, 152, 165, 179).
- Cross-check vs the recorded companion student's book (g02-student.pdf): TOTAL DISAGREEMENT — this is a record correction. The guide (2021, Ministry of Education, Science and Technology, integrated curriculum of 2077 BS) keys to a Grade 2 workbook that is not held: 44 themed lessons with Birendra, Riyana, Ruby, Ram Karki, Laxmi and Tina. The held student's book is the 2004 CDC textbook (second edition 2006, reprint 2011, Ministry of Education) with 12 units built around Pukar, Nima and Preeti. No unit, lesson, character exercise or page reference is shared between the two files; neither book mentions the other. The two files are a mismatched pair from two different curriculum generations, and any filing that treats this guide as the companion/answer key to the held Grade 2 student's book would be wrong.

## Grade 3 — MY ENGLISH BOOK Grade 3 (student's book)

### Identity block
- Title as printed: cover "MY ENGLISH BOOK / Grade 3", with the authors named on the cover itself; the running footer throughout is "MY ENGLISH BOOK, Grade 3". The record's wording "My English Book Grade 3" matches. There is no separate title page (PDF p.1 is the cover, PDF p.2 is the imprint page). The Contents page (PDF p.4) is headed "Content" (singular).
- Publisher: Government of Nepal, Ministry of Education, Curriculum Development Centre, Sanothimi, Bhaktapur, NEPAL (cover and imprint, PDF pp.1–2).
- Year / edition / ISBN as printed: ISBN 978-999-33-708-9-4 (imprint). "First edition: 2005 / Reprint: 2011" (no second edition is claimed). The imprint carries the CDC approval note for printing/distribution by the private sector in 32 districts of the Eastern and Western Development Region for academic year 2068 BS under the 'School Textbook Printing and Distribution Directives - 2067 BS', a blank printer/price block, and the printer's exchange note for printing errors.
- Developers/authors as printed: cover "Authors: Pramod Kumar Shah / Bishnu Prasad Parajuli". The Preface (PDF p.3) confirms the book was "originally written by Pramod Kumar Shah and Bishnu Prasad Parajuli" and "edited by the Curriculum Development Centre"; layout design by Navindra Rajbhandari and Himalaya Gautam; illustrations by Abindra Man Shrestha.
- Curriculum/series notes: third book of the CDC primary English series begun in 2003 (Preface). Format changes from Grades 1–2: units are longer, every unit ends with a crossword or puzzle, a chant and a riddle, units carry "Tips to/for the teacher" boxes in English, and the Nepali teacher-direction lines of Grades 1–2 largely disappear (one remains in Unit 6, p.46).
- Extent: 88 PDF pages = cover (PDF 1) + imprint (PDF 2) + Preface (PDF 3) + Content (PDF 4) + printed pp.1–84 (PDF 5–88). Printed p.84 is the last Word list page; there is no back cover in the file.
- Printed↔PDF offset: printed = PDF − 4. Established from the Content list (Unit 1 at printed p.1 = PDF 5; Listening Texts 80; Word List 81) and the printed folios, which match this offset throughout to p.84 (PDF 88).

### Unit-by-unit summary
- Unit 1 LILU'S DIARY (pp.1–9): telling the time fully — o'clock, six thirty, quarter to ten, quarter past, half past, minutes past/to from a substitution table ("It is a quarter past six"), long hand/short hand questions; a classroom-clock chant; the reading text of Lilu's daily routine (gets up at six, bath and milk at six thirty, homework until nine, meal at nine, school dress at half past nine, school at a quarter to ten) with discussion and pairwork; writing times in words (including the inconsistent "09.45" among colon times — see Slips); in the morning/afternoon/evening/at night; a numbers crossword; "Hickery, Dickery, Dock"; the "What's the time, Mr Wolf?" game; and a clock riddle.
- Unit 2 WHAT DO YOU LIKE? (pp.10–17): likes and alternatives — "Do you like tea or coffee? I like tea / I like both", pens or pencils, maths or English; the folk tale of the man with two wives (young wife pulls out his white hair, old wife his black hair, until he has none) told as a reading text with discussion, pairwork and a matching exercise whose answer lines are scrambled (see Slips); sound sets (white/kite/night…, hair/care/pair…); an act-out dialogue (football or volleyball, tennis or cricket); capital-letter completion carried over from Grade 2; alphabetical order (c-words); "Is this a cow or a dog?" alternative questions; a word-search for number words (fourteen, fifteen, eighteen, twenty, forty, ninety); the "White sheep" chant; a miming game; and a timetable riddle.
- Unit 3 WHERE IS MY PENCIL? (pp.18–24): prepositions in front of, beside, behind, on top of, over, between in a car-scene dialogue; the story of Pemba, who hunts everywhere for her pencil (not in her bag, on the table, under the bed, in the drawer) and finds it in her own mouth where she put it while combing her hair, with discussion and a preposition cloze (on, in front of, under, between); write-about-yourself frame; a listen-and-answer on/in discrimination exercise (scripts in Listening Texts, p.80); capital letters/full stops/question marks punctuation practice; a yes/no class survey; numbers in words to three figures (514 five hundred and fourteen, 564–799); the "Hockey-Cockey" chant; and a kite riddle.
- Unit 4 WHY ARE YOU LATE? (pp.25–33): apologies and directions — "I'm sorry I'm late today. Why are you late, Chintu? I forgot the time, miss. That's all right for today. Take your seatt, please" (sic); borrowing ("May I borrow your pen, please? I'm sorry. I have no extra pens"); asking the way ("Excuse me! Can you tell me the way to the temple, please? Turn left. Go straight… on your right. You're welcome") with dialogue completion and five direction-writing tasks (cinema hall, post office, swimming pool, school, police station); whose-questions with mine/hers/yours/his in a chant and a role play (pen, book, cap, umbrella; "It's Chankhey's"); a pencil-sharpener crossword (the word "pencilsharpener" written as one vertical word); and a table riddle.
- Unit 5 CAN YOU? (pp.34–40): can/can't with enough — "Can you touch the ceiling, Prashant? No, I can't. I'm not tall enough to touch it", strong enough, "I don't know how to fly/drive it"; true/false ticking and why-questions on the dialogue; the "Who can see the wind?" chant (Neither I nor you…); a languages dialogue (speak Chinese/French/Japanese/Nepali with country–language matching: China–Chinese, Nepal–Nepali, France–French, Japan–Japanese); read-and-copy true sentences about animals' abilities; can-you pairwork (climb a tree, fly a kite, play chess, write a letter in English, speak German); a duck riddle; the "What is Chintu?" animal-guessing game (a donkey); permission can ("Can I sit here? Yes, of course. Can I have some biscuits? No, I'm sorry. You can't… some selroti? Oh, sure"); and a listening tick/cross on what Puspa can and cannot do (scripts p.80 — the name is spelt "Puspa" there and "Pushpa" in the dialogue, see Slips).
- Unit 6 A PICNIC (pp.41–48; unit heading printed "A PICINIC" — see Slips): comparatives — a months chart (January cold, February less cold, June hot, "July is hotter than June") with hotter/colder/longer/shorter questions; the picnic story: last Monday's holiday picnic on the bank of the Kamala river — Miss Gita's class reach the bank at 9, Ali feels hot and swims, Pemba feels thirsty, Anu and Bindu feel sleepy and lie on the hot sand, the boys swim and play until 11, cook the food, and the girls wake when it is ready and apologise — with discussion and anagram words (cold, happy, swim, cooked, hungry); a make-sentences timetable of yesterday's actions (woke up, drank tea, left home, reached, played and swam, started cooking, ate, washed the dishes, came back home); yes/no do-you questions; tag-question dialogue ("It's a nice day, isn't it… So do I"); month sentence completions; fruit grouping by season; a picture-number puzzle (with the book's one Nepali teacher note, on consonant clusters, p.46); instructions for making a book of your own; the "I hear thunder" chant; a listen-and-finish story (the thirsty crow, script p.80, printed unfinished with "…"); and a river riddle.
- Unit 7 HELLO! (pp.49–56): telephoning and past simple — a phone dialogue ("Hello! Lilu speaking… What did you do yesterday? I did my maths homework. Did you draw pictures? No, I didn't") with completion dialogue and ask-your-friend past questions (when did you get up, did you help your mother, did you come by bus); the story of Bina's dog Pangre, left at home, who jumps on a chair, pulls off the table-cloth and breaks the vase, and is forgiven when he kisses her leg, with negative/affirmative verb completions (didn't go, jumped, pulled, didn't look after); an alphabetical-order table exercise; "Is this an apple or a mango?" alternative questions in the present continuous; a colour-of-things table (milk white, blood, crow, grass…); same-or-different picture writing; occupation sentences (drives a car — driver; flies an aeroplane; cooks food; works in the field; cuts hair); a crossword; the "Hello, Pemba, hello, Lilu!" chant; and an elephant riddle.
- Unit 8 BIRTHDAY (pp.57–63): letters and polite requests — Anu's thank-you letter to Ali (8, Lakeside, Pokhara; 21st July 2005; the present was the book "Muna"; the party will be on August 12th because her father is away) with discussion; a birthday dialogue ("When is your birthday, Ali? 10th June. It's today, isn't it? Happy birthday to you"); fill-ins from a word box (printed scrambled — see Slips); an invitation-letter completion (Thimi, Bhaktapur-17); "Could you open the windows, please? Yes. Of course."; map directions to Anu's house from the bus station ("Shehas invited Ali, too" — see Slips); how-can-I-get-to questions (stadium, school, hospital, temple, library); classroom adjective lists (small, big, long, short, black, white, hard, soft); capitalising days and months with the printed note "Always begin the names of days and months with a capital letter"; a crossword; the "I had a little party" chant (I, Myself and Me); and a heart/earth riddle.
- Unit 9 A VILLAGE GIRL (pp.64–71): future with will — opening dialogues ("Tomorrow is holiday. What will you do? I will go to market… I may be at home"); the Gauri story: fourteen-year-old Gauri is sent to sell her mother's milk, daydreams on the way (sell the milk, buy hens, hens lay eggs, eggs give more hens, "I'll be a rich girl"), turns at a man's call, and the pot falls and breaks — with discussion and matching; past-vs-future sentence completions ("I did not call you yesterday but I will call you tomorrow"); a will substitution table (go for a walk, make some cakes, play in the garden, swim in the river, get up early, go fishing); a plans dialogue with probably ("I will probably wash my clothes"); change-the-form (went = will go; ate, danced, played, washed, looked); sentence unscrambling; a read-and-write-about-yourself model (Lilu: birthday 10th March, "I will be nine years old next March", Jana Jyoti Primary School, Dada Gaun-7, Kaski); make-small-words-from-big-words (birthday, teacher, carpenter, football, computer); where-are-they/what-are-they-doing picture writing; a crossword; and a shoe riddle.
- Unit 10 A SPORTS DAY (pp.72–79): past-tense reading and review — the sports day text (three teams: Anand red, Anu blue, Ruchi yellow; Anu wins the 100 m race, the red team wins the sack race, and Ruchi's team comes last in the "marble and spoon race" — the questions call it the "egg and spoon race", see Slips) with questions, team matching and true/false listening; a skipping chant; do/does-like survey tables (swimming, singing, reading, playing, skipping, jumping, dancing) and like/don't like -ing lists; a number-trick puzzle dialogue; a past-conversation ("Nima, where were you yesterday? … It was a wedding party… my uncle's… I ate, and danced a lot"); a miming game of yesterday's actions; listening true/false on pictures (scripts p.80); colouring; the "Pussy Cat, Pussy Cat" chant (London and the Queen); and a snail riddle ("London, Paris, Moscow, Rome, Where ever I go, I'm always at home").
- Listening Texts (p.80): teacher-read scripts for Unit 3 ex.5, Unit 5 ex.13 (Puspa's abilities), Unit 6 ex.11 (the thirsty crow, breaking off mid-story with "…") and Unit 10 ex.8.
- Word List (pp.81–84): four-page alphabetical vocabulary list (about/aeroplane to zoo), reusing the Grades 1–2 list format and several of its entries.

### Grammar spine
The first book in the series to work in longer texts, but points still arrive as frames inside units, in this order: time expressions and clock language with present-simple routines (does she…? questions in Unit 1 pairwork) → do you like…or…? with like + noun and both → prepositions extended (beside, between, on top of, over) with past-simple narration in the Pemba story (was, did not find, pulled, laughed) and punctuation (capital letters, full stops, question marks) → apologies, may I borrow…?, can you tell me the way…? directions, and whose…? with possessive pronouns mine/hers/his/yours and 's possessives → can/can't for ability with too/enough ("not tall enough to touch it") and know how to, plus can for permission → comparatives (hotter than, colder, longer, shorter, less cold) with past narration and do-you habits questions and isn't-it tags with so do I → did/didn't past questions and answers in phone talk and the Pangre story → letter formats, dates and ordinals (21st July, 10th June, August 12th), could you…please requests, adjective sets → future will/'ll with may and probably, contrasted systematically against past forms (yesterday…but…tomorrow), and will-go change-the-form drills → Units 9–10 consolidate past vs future and do/does with like + -ing. No passive, no reported speech, no conditionals, no present perfect, no relative clauses anywhere in the book.

### Honest negatives
- No phonics or spelling system: sound sets appear twice (Unit 2) and alphabetical order is practised, but there is no letter-sound teaching, no blending and no dictation.
- No present perfect, no past continuous (Grades 2's was…ing is not continued), no going-to future, no comparative of adjectives beyond hot/cold/long/short in Unit 6's one chart (no -er spelling rules, no more/most, no superlatives except the listening text's "Everest is the highest mountain in the world", p.80, which is never taught), no articles teaching, no countable/uncountable work.
- Reading texts are short: the longest (picnic, Gauri, Pangre) run to roughly 150 words; there are no poems beyond chants and the two rhymes (classroom clock, Who can see the wind), no plays, and no non-fiction text other than the two letters in Unit 8.
- No answer keys, no tests or revision section as such (Unit 10 recycles), no grammar summaries or tables of forms anywhere — a learner who misses the frame in its unit has nowhere in the book to look it up.
- The riddles that end every unit are printed without answers anywhere in the student's book.

### Slips and errors as printed
- Unit 6 heading (p.41) is printed "A PICINIC" where the Content list (PDF p.4) has "A PICNIC" — the unit's own title is misspelt in the book.
- p.25 (Unit 4): "Take your seatt, please." — "seatt" for "seat".
- p.57 (Unit 8 letter): the date is "21st July 2005" and the party "will be on August 12th", yet the dialogue on p.58 is built around "10th June… It's today, isn't it?" for Ali's birthday — the two birthday dates in one unit do not belong to the same event and are never reconciled.
- p.58 (Unit 8, ex.2 fill-ins): the word box is printed "21st may When Today Thank you birthday" in scrambled order with "may" in lower case, against the book's own capital-letter rule for months printed on p.62.
- p.60 (Unit 8, map text): "Shehas invited Ali, too." — missing space.
- p.72 vs p.73 (Unit 10): the text's third race is the "marble and spoon race" but question (g) asks "Who came last in the egg and spoon race?" — text and questions name different races; the true/false item on p.73 likewise says "sack and spoon race".
- p.75 (Unit 10 puzzle): Prashant's name is spelt "Prashat" in his last speech turn, and his explanation stops mid-sentence: "The answer is always the first two digits of the final number:" — the worked explanation is truncated and, as printed, the trick is never actually explained.
- p.4 (Unit 1, ex.4): the write-in-words list mixes forms — "09.45" with a full stop among colon times (07:00, 06:45, 12:00, 11:15, 02:30, 11:25).
- p.12 (Unit 2 matching): the A/B matching lines are printed so that "The old man — liked black hair / The young wife — liked white hair / The old wife — did not like white hair…" — as laid out, the old man is matched to having two wives only in the last line's jumble; the exercise's pairings contradict the story on p.11 if read straight down (the young wife liked black hair, the old wife white).
- Pushpa/Puspa: the Unit 5 dialogue (p.40) names the girl "Pushpa" while the Listening Texts (p.80) and the ex.13 rubric call her "Puspa".
- Word list (pp.81–84) repeats the series' list faults: "falm", "knite", "pinapple" (p.83), "grand mother" split across lines, and two entries for "sister".

### Copy completeness and cross-check
- Folio verdict: complete. Printed folios run 1–84 unbroken on PDF pp.5–88; no missing or duplicated leaves; the file ends on the Word List's last page (p.84) and has no back cover.
- Content vs actual units: all 10 contents entries match the unit openings in number, title and page (LILU'S DIARY 1, WHAT DO YOU LIKE? 10, WHERE IS MY PENCIL? 18, WHY ARE YOU LATE? 25, CAN YOU? 34, A PICNIC 41, HELLO! 49, BIRTHDAY 57, A VILLAGE GIRL 64, A SPORTS DAY 72), except that Unit 6's own heading misspells its title (see Slips); the Listening Texts (80) and Word List (81) entries also match.
- Cross-check vs the recorded companion, Teacher's Guide My English Book Grade 3 (g03-teacher.pdf): UNLIKE the Grade 1 and 2 pairs, this guide IS the companion of this book — the CDC teacher's guide (First edition: 2008, Bagmati Offset Press; written by Bishnu Prasad Parajuli, Madhu Upadhaya, Bharat Babu Khanal and Arjun Lamichhane) to this exact Grade 3 text, one edition cycle earlier than this student's-book 2011 reprint. Unit order, titles and exercise numbering agree throughout (the guide's per-unit period tables reproduce this book's exercise lists exactly; its two page references, to "page 80 of the text book", land on this copy's Listening Texts page, and the listening texts it reprints match this copy word for word). The guide keys nearly everything the student's book leaves open — including ALL TEN riddles (Watch, Timetable, Kite, Table, Duck, river, elephant, heart→earth, shoes, Globe) and a "possible ending" for the truncated crow story — so the answerless items in this book are answered in the pair. It even flags two of this book's faults itself: the Unit 10 marble/egg race is keyed "marble (to be corrected in the text book)", and a boxed note says Unit 10's 2nd–4th picture sets are out of order ("The pictures of the boy and the girl need to be swapped"). Guide-side errors against this book: its Pushpa listening key inverts items 7–8, its nationality key prints "Nepal=Nepalese" against this book's "Nepal – Nepali" pattern, and it wobbles on names (Prerna/Prerana, Pushpa/Puspa, Prashanta). Full detail in the guide's own filing (bandA-g03-teacher.md).
